1. Eligibility Screening Against Policy
Screening applies the trusted table verbatim. P1 (quality 84, defects 4), P2 (97, 1), P3 (80, 3)
and P4 (81, 0) all meet quality at least 80 and sit at or under the 5-defect ceiling, so the whole
pool qualifies. Margins differ: P4 clears the ceiling by 5 counts, P2 by 4, P3 by 2, P1 by only 1.
P1 is therefore one defect from disqualification, a robustness caveat worth recording even
though eligibility today is unaffected. Quality spans 80 to 97, with P2 highest and P3 exactly
at the threshold.
2. Energy Ranking and Gap Arithmetic
On the shared acceptance run the ranking is P2 at 12 kWh, then P3 at 52, P1 at 56 and P4 at
58. Policy minimizes energy among qualifying candidates, so P2 leads outright and the
minimum is unique, leaving no tie-break. Gap arithmetic: P3 trails P2 by 40 kWh, roughly 4.3
times the leader's run; P1 by 44 kWh, about 4.7 times; P4 by 46 kWh, about 4.8 times.
Because every module serves the same fixed flow and head and shares one test basis, these
gaps are genuine efficiency differences, not duty or measurement artifacts.
3. Defect and Quality Trade-off; Open Questions
Quality and defects do not co-vary simply: P2 pairs the best quality (97) with one defect,
while P4 pairs a mid index (81) with zero defects. The written policy treats defects only as a
pass/fail ceiling and energy as the sole optimizer, so P4's zero-defect record does not change
the ranking, but it is the clearest secondary signal in the table. Open questions for
verification: (i) should defects carry weight beyond the ceiling, given P4 alone is defect-free;
(ii) is P1's 4-defect count stable or near retest; (iii) are quality indices independently
measured. Cost and reliability stay unverified.

Interpreting the Energy Gaps

schedule, maintenance burden and field reliability. They are unverified for all four candidates,

[DELIVERED TEXT SEEN BY B AND C]
1
WO-03 Compute Node Package Selection:
Energy-First Decision Brief
Executive summary
All four compute packages qualify: quality indices 87–98 clear the floor of 80 and defect
counts 0–4 sit under the ceiling of 5, so reject_all remains idle. On the shared acceptance
basis, measured energy ranks P3 at 10 Wh, P2 at 22, P1 at 28 and P4 at 49. The minimum is
unique: P3 leads P2 by 12 Wh and P4 by 39 Wh, so the candidate-ID tie-break is idle and the

Qualification screen and defect margins
Against the trusted table, every package passes both thresholds. Quality indices run 87 (P1),
95 (P2), 97 (P3) and 98 (P4), all above the floor of 80. Raw defect counts run 0 (P1), 0 (P2), 4
(P3) and 2 (P4), all under the ceiling of 5. Margins matter for robustness: P1 and P2 hold five
spare defect slots, P4 three, and P3 one — the thinnest margin in the field, though the
measured count of 4 satisfies the ceiling outright. No candidate fails on either axis, so
reject_all stays idle and all four enter the energy ranking.
Energy ranking and gap analysis
On the single shared basis — same workload, output requirement and test conditions —
energy is the only variable the policy minimizes. Ascending, the ranking reads P3 10 Wh, P2
22, P1 28, P4 49. Successive gaps are 12 Wh (P3 to P2), 6 Wh (P2 to P1) and 21 Wh (P1 to P4);
the full winner-to-last spread is 39 Wh. The minimum is unique, so the candidate-ID tie-break
stays idle. The 12 Wh lead is the smallest margin that matters: a re-measurement lifting P3
above 22 Wh would hand the win to P2, while P4 would need to shed 39 Wh to contend.
Trade-offs: quality, defects, energy
The figures show what each Wh buys. P3 concedes four defects and one quality point (97
versus 98) to P4 while saving 39 Wh. P2 is the zero-defect minimizer: no defects at 22 Wh,
still 12 Wh behind the winner. P1 pairs zero defects with the field's lowest quality (87) at 28
Wh. A derived ratio, energy per quality point, favors P3: 0.10 against 0.23 for P2, 0.32 for P1
and 0.50 for P4; the policy never asks for it. P4 leads only the quality sort (98, 97, 95, 87), but
under the current energy-first rule that leadership is not decisive.
Unverified factors and open questions
Cost, delivery, maintenance and field reliability are unmeasured for every candidate, so
treating any as decisive would be speculation. Two open questions stand. First, defect
margin: P3 qualifies with one slot to spare, so a policy that weights defects, or a
re-measurement adding defects, is the main route by which the pick could change; a
quality-first policy would instead favor P4 at 98. Second, basis stability: all values come from
one acceptance batch, so batch-to-batch variance is unmeasured. The next analyst should
re-derive eligibility and ranking from the table first, and audit every figure pixel against its
stated values.
Recommendation
P3
P3 is the unique lowest-energy qualifier at 10 Wh on the shared acceptance basis — 12 Wh
ahead of P2 and 39 Wh ahead of P4 — and clears both thresholds (quality 97, defects 4). The
only route to a different pick is a policy change, such as quality-first weighting, which would
favor P4 (98); that is not the current client rule. Unverified cost, delivery and reliability factors
cannot move the ranking.

Policy Screening of All Four Candidates
The trusted table gives quality 98 (P1), 86 (P2), 89 (P3) and 100 (P4), all above the 80 floor
with margins of 18, 6, 9 and 20. Raw defect counts read 1, 5, 1 and 5 against the ceiling of 5,
so every candidate qualifies. Two margins deserve note: P2 and P4 use all five defect slots
and have zero spare, while P1 and P3 carry four spare slots each. Because qualification is
binary and all four pass, the policy's energy-minimization step decides the pick; no tie-break
is needed since the lowest energy is unique.
Energy Ranking and Measured Gaps
Measured energy per identical acceptance batch ranks P1 6 kWh, P3 14, P2 40 and P4 67.
Successive gaps are 8, 26 and 27 kWh, and the winner-to-last spread is 61 kWh, more than
ten times P1's own draw. Energy-per-quality ratios order the same way: P1 0.06, P3 0.16, P2
0.47 and P4 0.67 kWh per quality point. P4's perfect quality index of 100 cannot offset
drawing more energy than any rival, because the policy is energy-first and quality only gates
qualification. The 26 kWh jump from P3 to P2 already separates the low-energy pair from the
high-energy pair.
Sensitivity and Open Questions
Three sensitivity facts matter. First, defect margin: P2 and P4 qualify with zero spare defect
slots, so any single additional measured defect disqualifies either, while P1 and P3 tolerate up
to four more. Second, quality margin: P2 sits closest to the floor at 86, six points above 80.
Third, batch scope: every figure comes from one acceptance batch on a shared basis, so
batch-to-batch variance is unmeasured. Cost, delivery, maintenance and field reliability
remain unverified and cannot move the energy-first ranking. These are recorded as open
questions, not facts.
Process Lessons Applied from Round 3
The round-3 assurance handover recorded figure-integrity failures in both prior documents: a
missing P2 bar drawn off scale in A's chart and misordered P1/P2 bars in B's figure, with a
missed cross-check. This brief applies that lesson: the accompanying chart is regenerated
directly from the trusted table, draws all four bars to one linear scale, and labels each height
with the tabulated value for pixel-versus-table verification. Reviewers should treat any drawn
value that departs from the table as a factual defect, not an open question.
Recommendation
P1

ahead of P4 — with quality 98, only one defect and four spare defect slots. Its 0.06

Section 1 heading: "Ordering by Measured Energy" body: "Under the single shared test basis every package delivers the same specified charging output, so kWh per acceptance batch is directly comparable. Because qualification is binary and all four candidates pass, no tie-break on candidate ID is needed — the minimum is unique."

Section 2 heading: "Robustness Margins and Spare Slots" body: "Quality margins over the 80 floor are +20 for P1, +15 for P2, +11 for P3 and +8 for P4. P2 combines the lowest measured energy with the second-best margin and four spare slots, so its selection is also robust on the binary screens. P4 sits exactly at the defect ceiling with zero spare slots: one additional measured defect would disqualify it outright. Executive summary:
"Zero candidates fail the qualification screen: quality 100, 95, 91 and 88 all clear the 80 floor, and defect counts 3, 1, 3 and 5 all sit at or under the ceiling of five, with P4 exactly at it. On the shared acceptance basis, measured energy ranks P2 lowest at 15 kWh, then P3 at 24, P1 at 44 and P4 at 64, so P2 is the client-correct selection, with successive gaps of 9, 20 and 20 kWh. This brief verifies eligibility, ranks the qualifiers, records margins and spare slots, separates measured facts from unverified items, and hands reviewers a six-step workflow for independent re-derivation."

~103 words. 

S1 "Ordering by Measured Energy":
"Every package delivers the same specified charging output under one identical acceptance test, so kWh per batch is directly comparable. The measured ladder is P2 15, P3 24, P1 44 and P4 64 kWh. P2 leads P3 by 9 kWh, P3 leads P1 by 20, and P1 leads P4 by 20; the winner-to-last spread is 49 kWh. Energy-per-quality ratios — 0.16 for P2, 0.26 for P3, 0.44 for P1 and 0.73 for P4 — preserve the same ordering. Because qualification is binary and all four candidates pass it, no candidate-ID tie-break is needed: the energy minimum is unique."

~100 words.

S2 "Robustness Margins and Spare Slots":
"Quality margins over the 80 floor are +20 for P1, +15 for P2, +11 for P3 and +8 for P4; spare defect slots under the ceiling are 2, 4, 2 and 0 respectively. P2 therefore combines the lowest measured energy with the second-best margin and the largest spare capacity, so its selection is robust on both binary screens. P4 sits exactly at the defect ceiling with zero spare slots: one additional measured defect would disqualify it outright. P3, at 24 kWh with two spare slots, is the next-lowest qualifier and the natural alternate only if a re-verification failure ever reopened the energy ranking."

~104 words. Note "only if... reopened" — conditional clearly labeled. Good.

S3 "Open Questions and Measurement Basis":
"The measured facts are limited to the trusted table: one quality index, one raw defect count and one energy figure per candidate, all on the identical output and load basis. Batch-to-batch variance is unmeasured, so a single acceptance batch cannot bound long-run energy. Cost, delivery schedule, maintenance burden and field reliability are unverified for every candidate and are deliberately excluded from the ranking, as are endorsements or vendor claims, none of which are supplied. Per the previous handover, any revision must recompute eligibility, ranking and gaps directly from the trusted table before any chart is drawn."

~99 words.

S4 "Zones of Sensitivity for Reviewers":
"Two sensitivities deserve verification attention. First, P4's zero spare slots make it the only candidate one measured defect from disqualification, so its raw defect count warrants a direct recheck. Second, the 9 kWh margin between P2 and P3 is the smallest gap in the ranking, so the P2 selection is the least robust to any measurement revision; recomputation from the table, not from a drawn chart, settles it. The six-step workflow diagram gives reviewers B and C an independent re-derivation path through screen, ordering, gaps, open items, spare-slot zone check and final selection."

~90 words. Hmm "six-step workflow diagram gives reviewers B and C an independent re-derivation path" — describes the figure, legitimate. No meta initials hint. Good.

Six-step verification workflow with the measured energy ladder on one linear scale (P2 15, P3

2
Ordering by Measured Energy
Every package delivers the same specified charging output under one identical acceptance
test, so kWh per batch is directly comparable. The measured ladder is P2 15, P3 24, P1 44
and P4 64 kWh. P2 leads P3 by 9 kWh, P3 leads P1 by 20, and P1 leads P4 by 20; the
winner-to-last spread is 49 kWh. Energy-per-quality ratios — 0.16 for P2, 0.26 for P3, 0.44 for
P1 and 0.73 for P4 — preserve the same ordering. Because qualification is binary and all four
candidates pass it, no candidate-ID tie-break is needed: the energy minimum is unique.
Robustness Margins and Spare Slots
Quality margins over the 80 floor are +20 for P1, +15 for P2, +11 for P3 and +8 for P4; spare
defect slots under the ceiling are 2, 4, 2 and 0 respectively. P2 therefore combines the lowest
measured energy with the second-best margin and the largest spare capacity, so its selection
is robust on both binary screens. P4 sits exactly at the defect ceiling with zero spare slots:
one additional measured defect would disqualify it outright. P3, at 24 kWh with two spare
slots, is the next-lowest qualifier and the natural alternate only if a re-verification failure ever
reopened the energy ranking.
Open Questions and Measurement Basis
The measured facts are limited to the trusted table: one quality index, one raw defect count
and one energy figure per candidate, all on the identical output and load basis.
Batch-to-batch variance is unmeasured, so a single acceptance batch cannot bound long-run
energy. Cost, delivery schedule, maintenance burden and field reliability are unverified for
every candidate and are deliberately excluded from the ranking, as are endorsements or
vendor claims, none of which are supplied. Per the previous handover, any revision must
recompute eligibility, ranking and gaps directly from the trusted table before any chart is
drawn.
Zones of Sensitivity for Reviewers
Two sensitivities deserve verification attention. First, P4's zero spare slots make it the only
candidate one measured defect from disqualification, so its raw defect count warrants a
direct recheck. Second, the 9 kWh margin between P2 and P3 is the smallest gap in the
ranking, so the P2 selection is the least robust to any measurement revision; recomputation
from the table, not from a drawn chart, settles it. The six-step workflow diagram gives
reviewers B and C an independent re-derivation path through screen, ordering, gaps, open
items, spare-slot zone check and final selection.
Recommendation
P2
Minimum measured energy decides the ranking: P2 at 15 kWh leads P3 by 9, P1 by 29 and P4
by 49 kWh, while also holding quality 95, one defect and four spare slots. Unverified cost,
delivery or reliability claims cannot override the measured minimum under the client policy.
